\displaystyle=\left|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(\xi_{k};b_{j+1})-\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(\chi_{k};b_{j})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(\xi_{k};b_{j+1})-\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(\chi_{k};b_{j+1})\right|+\left|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(\chi_{k};b_{j+1})-\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(\chi_{k};b_{j})\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}T_{i}^{\star}(\beta_{k};b_{j+1})|\,\delta+\left|\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{2}T_{i}^{\star}(\chi_{k}\mid T_{1}^{\star}(\gamma_{j}))\right||\partial_{x_{1}}T_{1}^{\star}(\gamma_{j})|\,\delta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{V}}{\ell_{V}^{3/2}}\,(1+|\beta_{k}|)\,\delta+\frac{L_{V}\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\,(1+|\chi_{k}|)\,\delta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{V}}{\ell_{V}^{3/2}}\,(1+|x_{i}|+\delta)\,\delta+\frac{L_{V}\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\,(1+|x_{i}|+\delta)\,\delta\,.

Thus,

\Bigl|\frac{\Delta_{j,k}}{\delta^{2}}\Bigr|\leq\Bigl(\frac{L_{V}}{\ell_{V}^{3/2}}+\frac{L_{V}\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\Bigr)\,(1+|x_{i}|+\delta)\,.

(70)

By (
67
)–(
70
), we conclude that for all
x_{i},x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,

0\leq\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\max\{\lambda_{k,j},\lambda_{k,j+1}\}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell_{V}}}\,,

|\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})|\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\max\{|\eta_{k,j}|,|\eta_{k+1,j}|\}\leq\frac{\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\,,

and

|\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})|=\Bigl|\frac{\Delta_{j,k}}{\delta^{2}}\Bigr|\leq\Bigl(\frac{L_{V}}{\ell_{V}^{3/2}}+\frac{L_{V}\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\Bigr)\,(1+|x_{i}|+\delta)\,.

This concludes the proof.
∎

The next result states that
T_{i}^{\star}
can be well approximated by
\widehat{T}_{i}
when the leaf variable is fixed.

Lemma C.9
.

Let (
R
), (
P1
), and (
RD

+

) hold. The estimate
\widehat{T}_{1}
of
T^{\star}_{1}
defined in (
64
) satisfies

\|\widehat{T}_{1}-T^{\star}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}\lesssim\,\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\exp(-R^{2}/2)+\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{{}^{\prime}})^{3}}\,\delta^{4}\,,

and

\|(\widehat{T}_{1}-T^{\star}_{1})^{\prime}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}\lesssim\,\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}R}\exp(-R^{2}/2)+\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell^{\prime}_{V})^{3}}\,\delta^{2}\,.

If (
P2
) also holds, then for any
b\in\mathcal{B}\cup\{b_{N+1}\}
, the estimate
\widehat{T}_{i}^{1}(\cdot;b)
defined in (
64
) satisfies

\|\widehat{T}_{i}(\cdot;b)-T_{i}^{\star}(\cdot;b)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}\lesssim\,\frac{1}{\ell_{V}}\exp(-R^{2}/2)+\frac{L_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}\,\delta^{4}\,,

and

\|\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(\cdot;b)-\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(\cdot;b)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}\lesssim\,\frac{1}{\ell_{V}R}\exp(-R^{2}/2)+\frac{L_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}\delta^{2}\,.

Proof.

It suffices to show the first pair of bounds; the second pair follows by the same argument. Following the proof of Theorem 5.6 in
55
, the pointwise bounds hold for
|x_{1}|\geq R
:

|\widehat{T}_{1}(x_{1})-T^{\star}_{1}(x_{1})|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\,(|x_{1}|-R)\,,\qquad|(\widehat{T}_{1}-T^{\star}_{1})^{\prime}(x_{1})|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\,.

Combining these and integrating by parts yields

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(-R,R)}|\widehat{T}_{1}-T^{\star}_{1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\rho_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(-R,R)}(|x_{1}|-R)^{2}\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})\lesssim\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,e^{-R^{2}/2}\,.

To bound the derivative term, we use the Mills ratio bound
(see, e.g.,
103
, Exercise 2.2)
to get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(-R,R)}|(\widehat{T}_{1}-T^{\star}_{1})^{\prime}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\rho_{1}\lesssim\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(-R,R)}\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})\lesssim\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}R}\,e^{-R^{2}/2}\,.

We now control the differences on the compact interval
[-R,R]
. By the definition of
\widehat{T}_{1}
and the mean value theorem, for any
x_{1}\in I_{j}
with
j\in[N]
, there exist
\zeta_{j},\eta_{j},\chi_{j}\in[b_{j},b_{j+1}]
such that

\displaystyle|\widehat{T}_{1}(x_{1})-T^{\star}_{1}(x_{1})|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\frac{T^{\star}_{1}(b_{j+1})-T^{\star}_{1}(b_{j})}{\delta}\,(x_{1}-b_{j})+T^{\star}_{1}(b_{j})-T^{\star}_{1}(x_{1})\Bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|(T_{1}^{\star})^{\prime}(\zeta_{j})\,(x_{1}-b_{j})-(T_{1}^{\star})^{\prime}(\eta_{j})\,(x_{1}-b_{j})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\big|(T_{1}^{\star})^{\prime\prime}(\chi_{j})\,(x_{1}-b_{j})\,(\zeta_{j}-\eta_{j})\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{4}\,\big|(T_{1}^{\star})^{\prime\prime}(\chi_{j})\big|^{2}\,.

Using the second derivative bound on
T^{\star}_{1}
in
Theorem

3.5
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[N]}\int_{I_{j}}|\widehat{T}_{1}-T^{\star}_{1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\rho_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{4}\sum_{j\in[N]}\int_{I_{j}}(1+|\chi_{j}|)^{2}\,\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3}}\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{4}\sum_{j\in[N]}\int_{I_{j}}(1+|x_{1}|+\delta)^{2}\,\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3}}\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3}}\,\delta^{4}\int(1+|x_{1}|^{2}+\delta^{2})\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})\lesssim\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3}}\,\delta^{4}\,.

Similarly, for the derivative difference, for some
\zeta_{j},\beta_{j}\in[b_{j},b_{j+1}]
(depending on
x_{1}
),

\displaystyle|(\widehat{T}_{1}-T_{1}^{\star})^{\prime}(x_{1})|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\frac{T^{\star}_{1}(b_{j+1})-T^{\star}_{1}(b_{j})}{\delta}-(T_{1}^{\star})^{\prime}(x_{1})\Bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle=\big|(T_{1}^{\star})^{\prime}(\zeta_{j})-(T_{1}^{\star})^{\prime}(x_{1})\big|^{2}

\displaystyle=\big|(T_{1}^{\star})^{\prime\prime}(\beta_{j})\,(\zeta_{j}-x_{1})\big|^{2}\leq\delta^{2}\,\big|(T_{1}^{\star})^{\prime\prime}(\beta_{j})\big|^{2}\,.

Applying the same second derivative bound,

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[N]}\int_{I_{j}}|(\widehat{T}_{1}-T_{1}^{\star})^{\prime}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\rho_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}\sum_{j\in[N]}\int_{I_{j}}(1+|\beta_{j}|)^{2}\,\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3}}\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}\sum_{j\in[N]}\int_{I_{j}}(1+|x_{1}|+\delta)^{2}\,\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3}}\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3}}\,\delta^{2}\,.

∎

We now prove our main approximation result.

Lemma C.10
.

Let (
R
), (
P1
), (
P2
), and (
RD

+

) hold. Then the approximator
\widehat{T}
defined in (
64
)–(
66
) satisfies

\displaystyle\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}+\frac{d}{\ell_{V}}+\frac{L_{V}^{\prime}}{\ell_{V}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\Bigr)\exp(-R^{2}/2)+\frac{d\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,\delta^{2}+\Bigl(\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{{}^{\prime}})^{3}}+\frac{dL_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}\Bigr)\,\delta^{4}\,.

Assume further that (
GR
) holds.
Then,

\|D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}+\frac{d}{\ell_{V}}+\frac{d\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\Bigr)\,\frac{\exp(-R^{2}/2)}{R}\\
+\Bigl[\Bigl(\frac{1}{d}+\frac{\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{2}}\Bigr)\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell^{\prime}_{V})^{3}}+\frac{L_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}+\frac{L_{V}^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{4}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}+\frac{(1+M_{2\gamma}(\rho_{1}))\,L_{\mathsf{GR}}^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}\Bigr]\,d\delta^{2}\,.

Remark C.11
.

(a)

The error bound for
\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}^{2}
arises from three sources:
(i) the error in approximating
T_{1}^{\star}
;
(ii) the error in approximating the first argument of
T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})
; and
(iii) the error in approximating the second argument of
T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})
.

(b)

The error bound for
\|D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}
consists of: (i) the approximation error for
DT^{\star}_{1}
; (ii)
the error in approximating the derivative
\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})
with respect to the first argument;
and (iii) the error in approximating the derivative with respect to the root variable, i.e.,
|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})|^{2}
.

(c)

Under the setting of
Theorem

2.10
, the star-separable map
T^{\star}=(T^{\star}_{1},T^{\star}_{2},\dotsc,T_{d}^{\star})
from
\mathcal{N}(0,I)
to
\pi^{\star}=\mathcal{N}(m^{\star},\Sigma^{\star})
has the explicit form

\displaystyle T^{\star}_{1}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=m_{1}+\sqrt{\sigma_{11}}\,x_{1}\,,

\displaystyle T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})

\displaystyle=m_{i}+\sigma_{i1}\,(\sigma_{11})^{-1/2}\,(T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1})-m_{1})+\sqrt{(\sigma^{ii})^{-1}}\,x_{i}\,,\qquad i\geq 2\,.

Therefore, (
GR
) is satisfied, and the approximation is exact up to the truncation error.

Given Lemma
C.10
, we can establish the following result, which shows the
existence
of a map
\widehat{T}
in our construction which satisfies the desired approximation guarantee.

Corollary C.12
.

Assume (
R
), (
P1
), (
P2
), (
RD

+

), and (
GR
) hold.
Then, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists a closed convex set
\Theta
of dimension
O((d^{2}/\epsilon^{2})\log(d/\epsilon^{2}))
and an affine parametrization
\theta\mapsto T_{\theta}
, where the implied constant depends polynomially on the constants
\ell_{V},L_{V},\ell_{V}^{\prime},L_{V}^{\prime},\overline{L},L_{\mathsf{GR}},M_{2\gamma}(\rho_{1})
and their inverses, where
M_{2\gamma}(\rho_{1})
is the
2\gamma
-th absolute moment of
\rho_{1}
, such that there exists
\hat{\theta}\in\Theta
satisfying

\|T_{\hat{\theta}}-T^{\star}\|_{L_{2}(\rho)}\leq\epsilon\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|D(T_{\hat{\theta}}-T^{\star})\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}\leq\epsilon\,.

Remark C.13
.

(a)

The proof of
Lemma

C.10
and
Corollary

C.12
can be adapted without changes to produce a map
\widehat{T}\in\underline{\mathrm{cone}}(\mathcal{M};\,\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id})
that approximates the optimal star-separable map
T^{\star}
and its gradient arbitrarily well in
L^{2}(\rho)
. Indeed, we can approximate
T^{\star}-\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id}
by some
\widehat{T}_{0}\in\underline{\mathrm{cone}}(\mathcal{M})
and set
\widehat{T}\coloneqq\widehat{T}_{0}+\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id}
.

(b)

Notice that the error rates for approximating
T^{\star}
and
DT^{\star}
are both of order
\epsilon
, which differs from the error rates in
55
for estimating univariate optimal transport maps.
In
55
, the functional value approximation bound scales as
\epsilon^{2}
, while the derivative approximation scales as
\epsilon
.
The dominant error here comes from approximating each
T_{i}^{\star}
in its second argument, i.e.,
|T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})|
, which scales as
\delta\asymp\epsilon
.

Proof.

Given
R
and
\delta
, the size of
\mathcal{M}_{0}
is
O(R/\delta)
, the sizes of
\mathcal{M}_{1}
,
\mathcal{M}_{2}
are each at most
O\!\left((d-1)\,(R/\delta)^{2}\right)
, while the sizes of
\mathcal{M}_{3}
,
\mathcal{M}_{4}
,
\mathcal{M}_{5}
are each
O\!\left((d-1)\,(R/\delta)\right)
. Therefore, the size of
\mathcal{M}
is
|\mathcal{M}_{0}|+|\mathcal{M}_{1}|+|\mathcal{M}_{2}|+|\mathcal{M}_{3}|+|\mathcal{M}_{4}|+|\mathcal{M}_{5}|=O(d\,\left(R/\delta\right)^{2})
. In what follows, we suppress polynomial dependence on
L_{V}
,
\ell_{V}
, etc. in the asymptotic notation
\lesssim
,
\asymp
, keeping only the dependence on the dimension
d
and the accuracy
\epsilon
.

Choose
R\asymp\sqrt{\log(d/\epsilon^{2})}
and
\delta\asymp\epsilon/\sqrt{d}
. Then, from the first bound of Lemma
C.10
, it is straightforward to see that
\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}\leq\epsilon
. Moreover, from the second bound on Lemma
C.10
, these choices yield
\|D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}\leq\epsilon
as well, which we will need for later reference.
∎

Finally, we introduce the following control on the second-order growth of
T_{i}^{\star}
w.r.t.
x_{1}
, which serves as the final ingredient to establish
Lemma

C.10
.

Lemma C.14
.

Assume (
R
), (
P1
), (
P2
), (
RD

+

), and (
GR
) hold.
Then, for all
i\geq 2
and
x_{i},x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\;\lesssim\;\frac{L_{\mathsf{GR}}}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,(1+|x_{i}|)^{\gamma}+\frac{L_{V}^{\prime}\,\overline{L}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3/2}\,\ell_{V}}\,(1+|x_{1}|)\,.

Proof.

By the chain rule,

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})

\displaystyle=\partial_{2}^{2}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1}))\,\bigl(\partial_{x_{1}}T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1})\bigr)^{2}+\partial_{2}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1}))\,\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1})\,.

By
Theorem

3.5
, the derivatives satisfy

\bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,,\quad\bigl|\partial_{2}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1}))\bigr|\leq\frac{\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}}\,,\quad\bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1})\bigr|\lesssim\frac{L_{V}^{\prime}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3/2}}\,(1+|x_{1}|)\,.

In addition, (
GR
) implies that

\bigl|\partial_{2}^{2}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1}))\bigr|\lesssim L_{\mathsf{GR}}\,(1+|x_{i}|)^{\gamma}\,.

Combining these bounds with
Theorem

3.5
and applying the triangle inequality yields

\bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|\;\lesssim\;\frac{L_{\mathsf{GR}}}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,(1+|x_{i}|)^{\gamma}+\frac{L_{V}^{\prime}\,\overline{L}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3/2}\,\ell_{V}}\,(1+|x_{1}|)\,.

∎

Now, we are ready to prove Lemma
C.10
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.10
.

Approximation error of
\widehat{T}-T^{\star}
.

First, we bound the approximation error
\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}
. For every
i\in[d]
and
j,k\in\{0,\ldots,N+1\}
, define

S_{j,k}^{(i)}\coloneqq\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:x_{1}\in I_{j},\,x_{i}\in I_{k}\}\,,

where
I_{j}=[b_{j},b_{j+1})
,
I_{k}=[b_{k},b_{k+1})
are the subintervals.

We adopt the shorthand:
\sum_{j}\coloneqq\sum_{j=0}^{N+1}
,
\sum_{k}\coloneqq\sum_{k=0}^{N+1}
,
\sum_{l}\coloneqq\sum_{l=0}^{N+1}
.
Write
\partial_{1},\partial_{2}
for differentiation with respect to the first and second arguments, respectively; e.g.,
\partial_{1}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid z_{1})=\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid z_{1})
and
\partial_{2}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid z_{1})=\partial_{z_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid z_{1})
. Recall
T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\coloneqq T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1}))
and
\rho=\mathcal{N}(0,I)=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{d}\rho_{i}
with
\rho_{1}=\cdots=\rho_{d}=\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

We expand

\displaystyle\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}

\displaystyle=\int\bigl|\widehat{T}_{1}(x_{1})-T^{\star}_{1}(x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})+\sum_{i=2}^{d}\int\bigl|\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)\,.

By the definition of
\widehat{T}_{i}
, we know that for
x_{1}\in I_{j}
,

\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})=(1-w_{j}(x_{1}))\,\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})+w_{j}(x_{1})\,\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j+1})\,,

where we recall that
w_{j}(x_{1})=\psi\bigl(\frac{x_{1}-b_{j}}{\delta}\bigr)\in[0,1]
and the convention that
b_{0}=b_{1}=-R
and
b_{N+1}=b_{N+2}=R
. In particular, we observe that when
j\in\{0,N+1\}
, then
\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})=\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})
.

Applying the triangle inequality followed by Jensen’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k}\int_{S^{(i)}_{j,k}}\bigl|\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j,k}\int_{S^{(i)}_{j,k}}\bigl|(1-w_{j}(x_{1}))\,\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})+w_{j}(x_{1})\,\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j+1})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j,k}\int_{S^{(i)}_{j,k}}w_{j}(x_{1})\,\big|\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})\big|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j,k}\int_{S^{(i)}_{j,k}}(1-w_{j}(x_{1}))\,\big|\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j+1})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j+1})\big|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j,k}\int_{S^{(i)}_{j,k}}w_{j}(x_{1})\,\bigl|T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j,k}\int_{S^{(i)}_{j,k}}(1-w_{j}(x_{1}))\,\bigl|T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j+1})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j,k}\int_{S^{(i)}_{j,k}}\big|\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})\big|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)+\sum_{j,k}\int_{S^{(i)}_{j,k}}\left|T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\right|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)\,.

Combining the bounds above gives

\displaystyle\int\bigl|\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\underbrace{\sum_{j,k}\int_{S^{(i)}_{j,k}}\big|\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})\big|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)}_{C_{i}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{j,k}\int_{S^{(i)}_{j,k}}\left|T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\right|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)}_{D_{i}}\,.

We bound
C_{i},D_{i}
separately. For
C_{i}
, by Fubini’s theorem and
Lemma

C.9
,

\displaystyle C_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}\int_{\{x_{1}\in I_{j}\}}\bigl|\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

(71)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}\rho_{1}(I_{j})\int\bigl|\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho_{i}(\mathrm{d}x_{i})

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{1}{\ell_{V}}\exp(-R^{2}/2)+\frac{L_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}\,\delta^{4}\Bigr)\sum_{j}\rho_{1}(I_{j})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\ell_{V}}\exp(-R^{2}/2)+\frac{L_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}\,\delta^{4}\,.

For
D_{i}
, using Fubini’s theorem and then
Lemma

C.2
, we derive that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{d}D_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=2}^{d}\sum_{j}\int_{\{x_{1}\in I_{j}\}}\bigl|T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}\int_{\{x_{1}\in I_{j}\}}\Bigl(\sum_{i=2}^{d}\int\bigl|T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid T_{1}^{\star}(b_{j}))-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1}))\bigr|^{2}\rho_{i}(\mathrm{d}x_{i})\Bigr)\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bigl\|\sum_{i\geq 2}|\partial_{1i}V|^{2}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\ell_{V}^{2}}\sum_{j}\int_{I_{j}}\bigl|T_{1}^{\star}(b_{j})-T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})\,.

Furthermore, since
T_{1}^{\star}
is
(1/\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}})
-Lipschitz by
Theorem

3.5
(i), we conclude that

\sum_{i=2}^{d}D_{i}\leq\frac{\bigl\|\sum_{i\geq 2}|\partial_{1i}V|^{2}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\sum_{j}\int_{I_{j}}|b_{j}-x_{1}|^{2}\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})\,.

(72)

Since the intervals
I_{j}
have length
\delta
when
j\in[N]
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\int_{I_{j}}|b_{j}-x_{1}|^{2}\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}+\int_{-\infty}^{-R}|x_{1}+R|^{2}\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})+\int_{R}^{\infty}|x_{1}-R|^{2}\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{2}+\exp(-R^{2}/2)\,.

Thus, (
72
) becomes

\sum_{i=2}^{d}D_{i}\lesssim\frac{\sum_{i\geq 2}\left\||\partial_{1i}V|^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,\bigl(\delta^{2}+\exp(-R^{2}/2)\bigr)\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{L_{V}^{\prime}}{\ell_{V}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\wedge\frac{d\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\Bigr)\,\bigl(\delta^{2}+\exp(-R^{2}/2)\bigr)\,,

(73)

where the last step uses (
RD
) since
\frac{1}{2}\,L_{V}^{\prime}-\frac{\bigl\|\sum_{i\geq 2}|\partial_{1i}V|^{2}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\ell_{V}}\geq 0
, and
\overline{L}\geq\max_{i\geq 2}{\|\partial_{1i}V\|_{L^{\infty}}}
.

Putting together (
71
) and (
73
), and the bound on
\|\widehat{T}_{1}-T^{\star}_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}^{2}
from
Lemma

C.9
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int\bigl|\widehat{T}_{1}(x_{1})-T^{\star}_{1}(x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})+\sum_{i=2}^{d}C_{i}+\sum_{i=2}^{d}D_{i}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\exp(-R^{2}/2)+\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{{}^{\prime}})^{3}}\,\delta^{4}+\Bigl(\frac{L_{V}^{\prime}}{\ell_{V}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\wedge\frac{d\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\Bigr)\,\bigl(\delta^{2}+\exp(-R^{2}/2)\bigr)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+(d-1)\,\Bigl(\frac{1}{\ell_{V}}\exp(-R^{2}/2)+\frac{L_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}\,\delta^{4}\Bigr)\,.

The right-hand side in the above display can be rearranged into

\displaystyle\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}+\frac{d}{\ell_{V}}+\frac{L_{V}^{\prime}}{\ell_{V}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\Bigr)\exp(-R^{2}/2)+\frac{d\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,\delta^{2}+\Bigl(\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{{}^{\prime}})^{3}}+\frac{dL_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}\Bigr)\,\delta^{4}\,,

as desired.

Approximation error of the gradients
D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})
.

We decompose

\displaystyle\|D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}

\displaystyle=\int\bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}T^{\star}_{1}(x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{T}_{1}(x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=2}^{d}\underbrace{\int\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)}_{\eqqcolon A_{i}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=2}^{d}\underbrace{\int\bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)}_{\eqqcolon B_{i}}.

Bounding
A_{i}
.

By (
67
), we derive for
x_{1}\in I_{j}
,

\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})=(1-w_{j}(x_{1}))\,\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})+w_{j}(x_{1})\,\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j+1})\,.

For each
i\geq 2
, applying the triangle inequality implies

\displaystyle\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-(1-w_{j}(x_{1}))\,\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})-w_{j}(x_{1})\,\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j+1})\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{}+\bigl|(1-w_{j}(x_{1}))\,\bigl(\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})-\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})\bigr)\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{}+\bigl|w_{j}(x_{1})\,\bigl(\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j+1})-\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j+1})\bigr)\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\underbrace{\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j+1})\bigr|^{2}}_{a_{ij}^{\star}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})-\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j+1})-\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j+1})\bigr|^{2}}_{\hat{a}_{ij}}\,.

Therefore,

\displaystyle A_{i}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j}\underbrace{\int_{\{x_{1}\in I_{j}\}}a_{ij}^{\star}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)}_{A_{ij}^{\star}}+\underbrace{\sum_{j}\int_{\{x_{1}\in I_{j}\}}\hat{a}_{ij}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)}_{\hat{A}_{i}}.

(74)

Thus, we can control
A_{i}
by the error
A_{ij}^{\star}
and the approximation error
\hat{A}_{i}
.

By the chain rule and
Theorem

3.5
(ii),

\bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|=\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{2}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1}))\bigr|\,\bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}T^{\star}_{1}(x_{1})\bigr|\lesssim\frac{L_{V}\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\,(1+|x_{i}|)\,,

(75)

where
\partial_{2}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid z_{1})\coloneqq\partial_{z_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid z_{1})
. Therefore, using the mean value theorem, we know that for
x_{1}\in I_{j}
,
j\in[N]
,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{L_{V}\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\,(1+|x_{i}|)\,|x_{1}-b_{j}|\leq\frac{L_{V}\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\,(1+|x_{i}|)\,\delta\,.

The above bounds holds when we replace
b_{j}
with
b_{j+1}
.
Therefore, Fubini’s theorem gives

\displaystyle A_{ij}^{\star}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{L_{V}\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\Bigr)^{2}\int_{\{x_{1}\in I_{j}\}}(1+|x_{i}|)^{2}\,\delta^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\frac{L_{V}^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{4}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,\int(1+|x_{i}|)^{2}\,\rho_{i}(\mathrm{d}x_{i})\,\delta^{2}\rho_{1}(I_{j})\lesssim\frac{L_{V}^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{4}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,\delta^{2}\rho_{1}(I_{j})\,.

On the other hand,
Theorem

3.5
(i) and the Mills ratio bound imply that for
j\in\{0,N+1\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\{x_{1}\in I_{j}\}}\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)\leq\int_{\{x_{1}\in I_{j}\}}\bigl(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell_{V}}}\bigr)^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\rho_{1}\left([R,\infty)\right)}{\ell_{V}}\lesssim\frac{\exp\left(-R^{2}/2\right)}{\ell_{V}R}\,.

Combining the previous two bounds yields that

\displaystyle\sum_{j}A_{ij}^{\star}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{L_{V}^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{4}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,\delta^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\rho_{1}(I_{j})+\frac{\exp\left(-R^{2}/2\right)}{\ell_{V}R}\lesssim\frac{L_{V}^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{4}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,\delta^{2}+\frac{\exp\left(-R^{2}/2\right)}{\ell_{V}R}\,.

(76)

For
\hat{A}_{i}
, observe that

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{i}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int\max_{j}{\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})-\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})\bigr|^{2}}\,\rho_{i}(\mathrm{d}x_{i})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\int_{-R}^{R}\max_{j}{\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})-\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})\bigr|^{2}}\rho_{i}(\mathrm{d}x_{i})}_{\eqqcolon\hat{A}_{i1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}\backslash[-R,R]}\max_{j}{\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})-\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};b_{j})\bigr|^{2}}\,\rho_{i}(\mathrm{d}x_{i})}_{\eqqcolon\hat{A}_{i2}}\,.

From
Lemma

C.8
,
|\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(\cdot\mid z_{1})|\leq 1/\sqrt{\ell_{V}}
. Using the Mills ratio and by
Theorem

3.5
(i), we have

\hat{A}_{i2}\lesssim\frac{\rho\left([R,\infty)\right)}{\ell_{V}}\lesssim\frac{\exp\left(-R^{2}/2\right)}{\ell_{V}R}\,.

(77)

As
Theorem

3.5
(i) shows
|\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})|\lesssim\frac{L_{V}}{\ell_{V}^{3/2}}\,(1+|x_{i}|)
for
x_{i}\in I_{k}
,
k\in[N]
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i}\mid b_{j})-\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i}\mid b_{j})\big|\leq\sup_{\tilde{x}_{i}\in I_{k}}\big|\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}T_{i}^{\star}(\tilde{x}_{i};b_{j})\big|\;\delta\lesssim\frac{L_{V}}{\ell_{V}^{3/2}}\,(1+|x_{i}|+\delta)\,\delta\,.

Hence,

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{i1}

\displaystyle=\int_{-R}^{R}\max_{j}{\bigl|\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})-\partial_{x_{i}}\widehat{T}_{i}^{1}(x_{i};b_{j})\bigr|^{2}}\,\rho_{i}(\mathrm{d}x_{i})

(78)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}\,\delta^{2}\int_{-R}^{R}(1+|x_{i}|+\delta)^{2}\,\rho_{i}(\mathrm{d}x_{i})\lesssim\frac{L_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}\,\delta^{2}\,.

Combining (
76
), (
77
), and (
78
), we see that

A_{i}\lesssim\hat{A}_{i1}+\hat{A}_{i2}\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{L_{V}^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{4}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}+\frac{L_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}\Bigr)\,\delta^{2}+\frac{\exp\left(-R^{2}/2\right)}{\ell_{V}R}\,.

(79)

Bounding
B_{i}
.

We now bound
B_{i}
:

\displaystyle B_{i}

\displaystyle=\int\bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{j\in\{0,N+1\}}\sum_{k}\int_{S^{(i)}_{j,k}}\bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)}_{\eqqcolon B_{i1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\sum_{j\in[N]}\sum_{k}\int_{S_{j,k}^{(i)}}\bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)}_{\eqqcolon B_{i2}}\,.

For
j\in\{0,N+1\}
, recall that

|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})|\leq\frac{\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}
.
In addition,
Lemma

C.8
shows
|\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})|\leq\frac{\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}
.
By the Mills ratio bound,

\displaystyle B_{i1}\lesssim\frac{\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus[-R,R]}\rho_{1}(\mathrm{d}x_{1})\lesssim\frac{\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\ell_{V}^{\prime}R}\exp(-R^{2}/2)\,.

(80)

Fix
j\in[N]
and
k\in\{0,\ldots,N+1\}
.
By the mean value theorem, there exists
\zeta_{j}\in I_{j}
such that

\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};\zeta_{j})=\delta^{-1}\,\bigl(T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j+1})-T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};b_{j})\bigr)
.
Applying the mean value theorem again together with (
67
) with
\eta_{k,j}=T_{i}^{\star}(b_{k};b_{j+1})-T_{i}^{\star}(b_{k};b_{j})
,
\Delta_{j,k}=\eta_{k+1,j}-\eta_{k,j}
, we see that for some
\zeta_{j}\in I_{j}
,

\displaystyle\bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(b_{k};\zeta_{j})-(x_{i}-b_{k})\,\frac{\Delta_{j,k}}{\delta^{2}}\Bigr|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(b_{k};x_{1})+\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(b_{k};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(b_{k};\zeta_{j})-(x_{i}-b_{k})\,\frac{\Delta_{j,k}}{\delta^{2}}\Bigr|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\bigl(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(\beta_{k};x_{1})-\tfrac{\Delta_{j,k}}{\delta^{2}}\bigr)\,(x_{i}-b_{k})+\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(b_{k};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(b_{k};\zeta_{j})\Bigr|

for some
\beta_{k}
between
x_{i}
and
b_{k}
.
By
Theorem

3.5
(ii),

|\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(\zeta^{\prime};x_{1})|\leq|\partial_{2}\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}^{\star}(\zeta^{\prime}\mid T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1}))|\,|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{1}^{\star}(x_{1})|\\
\lesssim\frac{L_{V}\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\,(1+|\zeta^{\prime}|)\leq\frac{L_{V}\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\,(1+|x_{1}|+\delta)\,.

In addition,
Lemma

C.14
implies

|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(b_{k};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(b_{k};\zeta_{j})|\lesssim\frac{L_{\mathsf{GR}}}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,(1+|x_{1}|+\delta)^{\gamma}\,\delta+\frac{L_{V}^{\prime}\,\overline{L}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3/2}\,\ell_{V}}\,(1+|x_{1}|+\delta)\,\delta\,.

Combining the above bounds and applying the triangle inequality together with

Lemma

C.8
, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl[\frac{L_{V}\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\,(1+|x_{i}|+\delta)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{L_{V}^{\prime}\,\overline{L}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3/2}\,\ell_{V}}\,(1+|x_{1}|+\delta)+\frac{L_{\mathsf{GR}}}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,(1+|x_{1}|+\delta)^{\gamma}\Bigr]\,\delta\,.

Therefore,

\displaystyle B_{i2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{S_{j,k}^{(i)}}|\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}^{\star}(x_{i};x_{1})-\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{T}_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})|^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

(81)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{L_{V}^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{4}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{S_{j,k}^{(i)}}(1+|x_{i}|+\delta)^{2}\,\delta^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle+\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3}\ell_{V}^{2}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{S_{j,k}^{(i)}}(1+|x_{1}|+\delta)^{2}\,\delta^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle+\frac{L_{\mathsf{GR}}^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\int_{S_{j,k}^{(i)}}(1+|x_{1}|+\delta)^{2\gamma}\,\delta^{2}\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{2}\,\Bigl[\frac{L_{V}^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{4}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}+\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3}\ell_{V}^{2}}+\frac{L_{\mathsf{GR}}^{2}\,(1+M_{2\gamma}(\rho_{1}))}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}\Bigr]\,.

Hence, combining (
80
) and (
81
) yields

\displaystyle B_{i}\lesssim\frac{\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\ell_{V}^{\prime}R}\exp(-R^{2}/2)+\delta^{2}\,\Bigl[\frac{L_{V}^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{4}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}+\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3}\ell_{V}^{2}}+\frac{(1+M_{2\gamma}(\rho_{1}))\,L_{\mathsf{GR}}^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}\Bigr]\,.

(82)

Finally, using
Lemma

C.9
together with (
79
) and (
82
),

\displaystyle\|D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}+\frac{d-1}{\ell_{V}}+\frac{d\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\Bigr)\,\frac{\exp(-R^{2}/2)}{R}+\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell^{\prime}_{V})^{3}}\,\delta^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Bigl[\frac{L_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}+\frac{L_{V}^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{4}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}+\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{3}\ell_{V}^{2}}+\frac{(1+M_{2\gamma}(\rho_{1}))\,L_{\mathsf{GR}}^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}\Bigr]\,(d-1)\,\delta^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{1}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}+\frac{d}{\ell_{V}}+\frac{d\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{2}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\Bigr)\,\frac{\exp(-R^{2}/2)}{R}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Bigl[\Bigl(\frac{1}{d}+\frac{\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{2}}\Bigr)\frac{(L_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}{(\ell^{\prime}_{V})^{3}}+\frac{L_{V}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{3}}+\frac{L_{V}^{2}\overline{L}^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{4}\ell_{V}^{\prime}}++\frac{(1+M_{2\gamma}(\rho_{1}))\,L_{\mathsf{GR}}^{2}}{(\ell_{V}^{\prime})^{2}}\Bigr]\,d\delta^{2}\,.

∎

C.3
The computational guarantees

In previous section, we explicitly construct a map
\widehat{T}\in\underline{\rm cone}(\mathcal{M};\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id})
that approximate the optimal star-separable map
T^{\star}
and its gradient arbitrarily well in
L^{2}(\rho)
. However, this construction requires knowledge of
T^{\star}
. In this section, we show that a good approximation to
T^{\star}
can by computed by solving a convex optimization problem.

Given the dictionary
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{1}\cup\mathcal{M}_{2}\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{M}_{5}
defined in (
62
), we search for the minimizer
T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}
of the KL divergence with respect to
\pi
over
\underline{\rm cone}(\mathcal{M};\,\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id})
, i.e.,

T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}=\argmin_{T\in\underline{\rm cone}(\mathcal{M};\,\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id})}\mathsf{KL}\left(T_{\#}\rho\parallel\pi\right).

(SSVI-opt)

The above optimization problem admits a unique minimizer.
The computational guarantees in
Theorem

C.15
are shown without invoking the explicit form of the maps in
\underline{\rm cone}(\mathcal{M};\,\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id})
, as we choose to work with (
SSVI-opt
).

The proof of
Theorem

C.15
involves two steps. First, by
Corollary

C.12
, we choose an “oracle” approximator
\widehat{T}\in\underline{\rm cone}\left(\mathcal{M};\,\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id}\right)
that approximates
T^{\star}
. Second, we show that
T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}
is close to the approximating map
\widehat{T}
in
L^{2}(\rho)
using the geometry of the problem (
SSVI-opt
). The result then follows by combining the bounds from the two steps.

Theorem C.15
.

Let (
R
), (
P1
), (
P2
), (
RD

+

), and (
GR
) be satisfied. Then, for each
\epsilon>0
, there exists a dictionary
\mathcal{M}
of size
O((d^{2}/\epsilon^{2})\log(d/\epsilon^{2}))
such that

\|T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}-T^{\star}\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}\lesssim\epsilon,

where
T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}
is the minimizer to (
SSVI-opt
) and the constants depend polynomially on
\ell_{V}
,
L_{V}
,
\ell_{V}^{\prime}
,
L_{V}^{\prime}
,
\overline{L}
,
L_{\mathsf{GR}}
,
M_{2\gamma}(\rho_{1})
and their inverses.

In the sequel, we will work with the following measures:
\pi^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}\coloneqq(T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}})_{\#}\rho
,
\hat{\pi}\coloneqq(\widehat{T})_{\#}\rho
. Define the finite-dimensional set

\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{M}}\coloneqq\left\{T_{\#}\rho:T\in\underline{\rm cone}\left(\mathcal{M};\,\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id}\right)\right\}\subset\mathcal{T}_{\rm star}\,.

The following result shows that the objective value of
\pi^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}
is close to that of
\hat{\pi}
, which is used to control the distance between
T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
\widehat{T}
in
Theorem

C.15
.

Lemma C.16
.

Under the setting of
Theorem

C.15
, there exists
\kappa>0
depending polynomially on
\ell_{V},\ell_{V}^{\prime},L_{V},L_{V}^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{KL}\left(\hat{\pi}\parallel\pi\right)-\mathsf{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}\parallel\pi\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\,\bigl((L_{V}\lor L_{V}^{\prime})\,\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}+\kappa^{2}\,\|D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}\bigr)\,.

Proof.

The first inequality is straightforward since
\hat{\pi}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{M}}
. To show the second inequality, we use the fact that
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{M}}\subset\mathcal{C}_{\rm star}
to obtain

\mathsf{KL}\left(\hat{\pi}\parallel\pi\right)-\mathsf{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}\parallel\pi\right)\leq\mathsf{KL}\left(\hat{\pi}\parallel\pi\right)-\mathsf{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}\parallel\pi\right)\,.

To conclude, it suffices to bound the right-hand side above.

Recall that
T^{\star}
(resp.
\widehat{T}
) denotes the star-separable map that pushes forward
\rho
to
\pi^{\star}
(resp.
\hat{\pi}
). By definition,
T^{\star},\widehat{T}\in\mathcal{T}_{\rm star}
.
Define
T_{t}\coloneqq(1-t)T^{\star}+t\widehat{T}
and
\mu_{t}\coloneqq{T_{t}}_{\#}\rho
, then
\mu_{0}=\pi^{\star}
and
\mu_{1}=\hat{\pi}
. We note that
\mu_{t}\in\mathcal{C}_{\rm star}
as
\mathcal{T}_{\rm star}
is convex. Recall (
51
) and (
52
), with
T_{0}=T^{\star}
and
T_{1}=\widehat{T}
.

By
Lemma

2.6
,
\nabla^{2}V(T_{t})\preceq[L_{V}\lor(L_{V}^{\prime}/2)]\,I
, and thus the second derivative of the potential energy is bounded by

\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathcal{V}(\mu_{t})}{\mathrm{d}t^{2}}\leq\left(L_{V}\lor(L_{V}^{\prime}/2)\right)\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}\,.

(83)

By
Theorem

3.5
and
Lemma

C.8
, we apply
Lemma

C.17
with
L_{1}=DT^{\star}
,
L_{2}=D\widehat{T}
,
c_{1}=\sqrt{1/L_{V}}\wedge\sqrt{1/L_{V}^{\prime}}=\sqrt{1/(L_{V}\vee L_{V}^{\prime})}
, and
c_{3}=\frac{\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}
to derive that

\|(DT_{t})^{-1}\|_{2}\leq\frac{c_{1}+\sqrt{d-1}\,c_{3}}{c_{1}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{c_{1}}+\frac{L_{V}^{\prime}}{2\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}\,c_{1}^{2}}\eqqcolon\kappa\,,

where the last inequality follows from
\frac{L_{V}^{\prime}}{2\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}\geq\frac{(d-1)\,\overline{L}}{\ell_{V}\sqrt{\ell_{V}^{\prime}}}=(d-1)\,c_{3}
by (
RD

+

) and
d\geq 2
. Therefore, by (
52
),

\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})}{\mathrm{d}t^{2}}\leq\kappa^{2}\,\|D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}\,.

(84)

Thus, combining (
83
) and (
84
) yields

\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{t}\parallel\pi\right)}{\mathrm{d}t^{2}}\leq\left(L_{V}\lor(L_{V}^{\prime}/2)\right)\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}+\kappa^{2}\,\|D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}\,.

(85)

Moreover, as

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}\left(\hat{\pi}\parallel\pi\right)-\mathsf{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}\parallel\pi\right)

\displaystyle=\left\langle[\nabla_{W}\mathsf{KL}\left(\cdot\parallel\pi\right)](\pi^{\star}),\,T-\id\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\pi^{\star})}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}s^{2}}\,\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{s}\parallel\pi\right)\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\,,

the optimality of
\pi^{\star}
and the derivative bound (
85
) imply that

\mathsf{KL}\left(\hat{\pi}\parallel\pi\right)-\mathsf{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}\parallel\pi\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\,\bigl((L_{V}\lor L_{V}^{\prime})\,\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}+\kappa^{2}\,\|D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}\bigr)\,.

∎

Proof of
Theorem

C.15
.

Let
\widehat{T}
be the approximation of
T^{\star}
in
\underline{\rm cone}(\mathcal{M};\,\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id})
, constructed in
Corollary

C.12
with
|\mathcal{M}|=O(\dim(\Theta))=O\left((d^{2}/\epsilon^{2})\log(d/\epsilon^{2})\right)
. Then
T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}},\widehat{T}\in\mathcal{T}_{\rm star}
. By the triangle inequality,

\|T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}-T^{\star}\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}\leq\|T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}-\widehat{T}\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}+\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}\,.

The second term is controlled by
Corollary

C.12
, recalling that

\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}\leq\epsilon\,.

(86)

It remains to bound the first term. By
Lemma

2.6
,
\pi
is
(\ell_{V}^{\prime}\wedge\ell_{V})
-log-concave. Then, by
Theorem

3.2
, the map
T\mapsto\mathrm{KL}(T_{\#}\rho\mathbin{\|}\pi)
is
(\ell_{V}^{\prime}\wedge\ell_{V})
-convex over the convex set
\underline{\rm cone}(\mathcal{M};\,\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id})\subset\mathcal{T}_{\rm star}
. Together with the optimality of
\pi^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}
, this yields

\frac{\ell_{V}^{\prime}\wedge\ell_{V}}{2}\,\|T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}-\widehat{T}\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}^{2}\leq\KL(\widehat{\pi}\mathbin{\|}\pi)-\KL(\pi^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\,.

(87)

In addition, by
Lemma

C.16
, there exists a constant
\kappa>0
depending polynomially on
\ell_{V},\ell_{V}^{\prime},L_{V},L_{V}^{\prime}
such that

\mathsf{KL}\left(\hat{\pi}\parallel\pi\right)-\mathsf{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}\parallel\pi\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\,\bigl((L_{V}^{\prime}\vee L_{V})\,\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}+\kappa^{2}\,\|D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}\bigr)\,.

Together with (
87
), this gives

\|T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}-\widehat{T}\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}^{2}\leq\frac{L_{V}^{\prime}\vee L_{V}}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}\wedge\ell_{V}}\,\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\ell_{V}^{\prime}\wedge\ell_{V}}\,\|D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\rho)}\,.

(88)

The first term is already controlled in (
86
). On the other hand, by
Corollary

C.12
,

\|D(\widehat{T}-T^{\star})\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}\leq\epsilon\,.

The above bound and (
86
) imply that
\|T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}-T^{\star}\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}\lesssim\epsilon
.
∎

We conclude this section with a technical lemma used in establishing
Lemma

C.16
.

Lemma C.17
.

For
i=0,1
, let
L_{i}\coloneqq D_{i}+u_{i}e_{1}^{\top}
, where
D_{i}
is a diagonal matrix and
u_{i}=(0,u_{i2},\ldots,u_{id})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For
t\in[0,1]
, define
L_{t}\coloneqq(1-t)L_{0}+tL_{1}
. Suppose further that
c_{1}I\preceq D_{0},D_{1}\preceq c_{2}I
and
\max\{\|u_{0}\|_{\infty},\|u_{1}\|_{\infty}\}\leq c_{3}
for some constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}>0
. Then,

\|L_{t}\|_{2}\leq c_{2}+\sqrt{d-1}\,c_{3}\qquad{\rm and}\qquad\|L_{t}^{-1}\|_{2}\leq\frac{c_{1}+\sqrt{d-1}\,c_{3}}{c_{1}^{2}}\,.

Proof.

Denote
D_{t}\coloneqq(1-t)D_{0}+tD_{1}
and
u_{t}\coloneqq(1-t)u_{0}+tu_{1}
. Then
L_{t}=D_{t}+u_{t}e_{1}^{\top}
is invertible. Therefore,

\|L_{t}\|_{2}\leq\|D_{t}\|_{2}+\|u_{t}e_{1}^{\top}\|_{2}\leq c_{2}+\sqrt{d-1}\,c_{3}\,.

On the other hand, by the Sherman–Morrison formula
(
95
)
,

L_{t}^{-1}=D_{t}^{-1}-\frac{D_{t}^{-1}u_{t}e_{1}^{\top}D_{t}^{-1}}{1+e_{1}^{\top}D_{t}^{-1}u_{t}}=D_{t}^{-1}-D_{t}^{-1}u_{t}e_{1}^{\top}D_{t}^{-1}\,,

since
e_{1}^{\top}D_{t}^{-1}u_{t}=0
(the first coordinate of
u_{t}
is
0
). Hence,

\|L_{t}^{-1}\|_{2}\leq\|D_{t}^{-1}\|_{2}+\|D_{t}^{-1}u_{t}\|\,\|e_{1}^{\top}D_{t}^{-1}\|\leq\frac{1}{c_{1}}+\frac{\sqrt{d-1}\,c_{3}}{c_{1}^{2}}\,,

because
D_{t}\succeq c_{1}I
,
\|u_{t}\|\leq\sqrt{d-1}\,c_{3}
, and
\|e_{1}^{\top}D_{t}^{-1}\|=\|D_{t}^{-1}e_{1}\|\leq 1/c_{1}
.
∎

C.4
Algorithm details

This subsection provides the details for computing the minimizer
T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}
of the problem (
SSVI-opt
) using the projected gradient descent algorithm. Recall that

T^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}}=\argmin_{T\in\underline{\rm cone}(\mathcal{M};\,\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id})}\mathsf{KL}\left(T_{\#}\rho\parallel\pi\right).

(89)

where
\underline{\rm cone}(\mathcal{M};\bm{\alpha}\,\mathrm{id})
denotes the augmented spiked cone defined in (
63
) with
\alpha_{1}=(L^{\prime}_{V})^{-1/2}
and
\alpha_{2}=\cdots=\alpha_{d}=L_{V}^{-1/2}
.

To resolve this, we show that (
SSVI-opt
) can be instead reformulated as a
finite-dimensional
,
strongly convex
optimization problem. To this end, we introduce the following set: let
\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\prime}
be the set of functions in
\mathcal{M}_{5}
with positive signs, that is

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\prime}&\coloneqq\Bigl\{x\mapsto\psi\Bigl(\frac{x_{1}-b}{\delta}\Bigr)\,e_{i}\,\,\Bigl|\,\,i\geq 2,\,b\in\mathcal{B}\Bigr\}\,.\end{split}

(90)

Define
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{M}_{0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{M}_{4}\cup\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\prime}
. We require that
\int T(x)\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)=0
for all
T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
. We can achieve this by centering each function and absorbing the constant shift in the vector
v
. Moreover, by construction, all functions in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
are linearly independent in the pointwise sense: no element of
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is a linear combination of the others almost everywhere with respect to
\rho
.

Let
\Theta\coloneqq\bigl(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\cup_{i=0}^{4}\mathcal{M}_{i}|}\times\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\prime}|}\bigr)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Then, (
SSVI-opt
) is equivalent to the following finite-dimensional,
(\ell_{V}\wedge\ell_{V}^{\prime})
-strongly convex optimization problem:

(\hat{\lambda},\hat{v})=\argmin_{(\lambda,v)\in\Theta}\KL\biggl(\Bigl(\bm{\alpha}\,\id+\sum_{T\in\cup_{i=0}^{4}\mathcal{M}_{i}}\lambda_{T}T+\sum_{\tilde{T}\in\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\prime}}\lambda_{\tilde{T}}\tilde{T}+v\Bigr)_{\#}\rho\biggm\|\pi\biggr)\,,

(91)

where
\Theta
is equipped with the norm
\|(\lambda,v)\|_{\Theta}\coloneqq\|\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}T+v\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}
. To see the equivalence, we first note that by direct calculation

\left\|(\lambda,v)\right\|_{\Theta}^{2}=\Bigl\|\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}T+v\Bigr\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}^{2}=\langle\lambda,Q\lambda\rangle+\langle v,v\rangle=\|\lambda\|_{Q}^{2}+\|v\|^{2}\,,

where
Q
is a positive semi-definite Gram matrix with entries
Q_{T,\tilde{T}}\coloneqq\langle T,\tilde{T}\rangle_{L_{2}(\rho)}
for
T,\tilde{T}\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
. In fact,
Q
is positive definite since functions in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
are linearly independent almost everywhere under
\rho
. As a result,
\|(\lambda,v)\|_{\Theta}=0
implies
(\lambda,v)=(0,0)
, i.e.,
\|\cdot\|_{\Theta}
is indeed a norm.

Define
T_{\lambda,v}\coloneqq\bm{\alpha}\id+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}T+v
. The set
\underline{\rm cone}\left(\mathcal{M};\,\bm{\alpha}\id\right)_{\#}\rho
endowed with the linearized optimal transport distance admits a natural isometric embedding into the space
\Theta
equipped with norm
\|\cdot\|_{\Theta}
, in the sense that

\displaystyle{\rm d}^{2}_{\rm LOT,\rho}\left(\mu_{\eta,u},\mu_{\lambda,v}\right)

\displaystyle=\Bigl\|\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}(\eta_{T}-\lambda_{T})T+u-v\Bigr\|_{L^{2}(\rho)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\eta-\lambda\right\|_{Q}^{2}+\|u-v\|_{2}^{2}=\left\|(\eta,u)-(\lambda,v)\right\|_{\Theta}^{2}\,.

This equivalence of norms, together with the discussion in
Section

3.1
, establishes the claimed equivalence between the lifted and finite-dimensional formulations.

We can now employ tools from finite-dimensional convex optimization to solve (
SSVI-opt
) with convergence guarantees. In particular, we compute the minimizer using projected gradient descent (PGD) as described in
Algorithm

1
; see, for example, Section 10.2 of
11
for a detailed discussion of its convergence properties.

Algorithm 1

Projected gradient descent over
\underline{\rm cone}\left(\mathcal{M};\,\bm{\alpha}\id\right)

Input:
Piecewise linear dictionary
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
and projection cone

\mathcal{K}\coloneqq\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\cup_{i=0}^{4}\mathcal{M}_{i}|}\times\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\prime}|}
. Constants
\alpha\coloneqq\ell_{V}\land\ell_{V}^{\prime}
,
L\coloneqq L_{V}\lor(L_{V}^{\prime}/2)
,
\Upsilon\coloneqq 9\delta^{-2}\,\bigl((L_{V}^{\prime})^{1/2}+(d-1)\,L_{V}^{1/2}\bigr)^{2}\,\|Q^{-1}\|_{2}
.

Output:

(\hat{\lambda},\hat{v})
.

Initialization:
Initialize
(\lambda^{(0)},v^{(0)})\in\Theta

for

t=0,1,2,3,\dotsc

do

\lambda^{(t+1)}=\text{proj}_{\mathcal{K},Q}\bigl(\lambda^{(t)}-\frac{1}{L+\Upsilon}\,Q^{-1}\nabla_{\lambda}\KL(\mu_{\lambda^{(t)},v^{(t)}}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\bigr)

v^{(t+1)}=v^{(t)}-\frac{1}{L+\Upsilon}\,\nabla_{v}\KL(\mu_{\lambda^{(t)},v^{(t)}}\mathbin{\|}\pi)

end

for

Theorem C.18

(Convergence results for
Algorithm

1
)
.

Let Assumptions (
R
), (
P1
), (
P2
), and (
RD

+

) be satisfied. Let
\Upsilon\coloneqq 9\delta^{-2}\,\bigl((L_{V}^{\prime})^{1/2}+(d-1)\,L_{V}^{1/2}\bigr)^{2}\,\|Q^{-1}\|_{2}
, and let
\{(\lambda^{(t)},v^{(t)})\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
denote the iterates generated by
Algorithm

1
. Then for
\kappa\coloneqq\frac{L_{V}\lor(L_{V}^{\prime}/2)+\Upsilon}{\ell_{V}\land\ell_{V}^{\prime}}
,

(i)

{\rm d}^{2}_{\rm LOT,\rho}(\mu_{\lambda(t),v(t)},\mu_{\hat{\lambda},\hat{v}})\leq\left(1-\kappa^{-1}\right)^{t}{\rm d}^{2}_{\rm LOT,\rho}(\mu_{\lambda(0),v(0)},\mu_{\hat{\lambda},\hat{v}})
.

(ii)

\KL(\mu_{\lambda(t),v(t)}\mathbin{\|}\pi)-\KL(\mu_{\hat{\lambda},\hat{v}}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leq(1-\kappa^{-1})^{t}\,\frac{(L+\Upsilon)}{2}\,{\rm d}^{2}_{\rm LOT,\rho}(\mu_{\lambda(0),v(0)},\mu_{\hat{\lambda},\hat{v}})
.

We first note that applying
Theorem

C.18
with
\theta^{(t)}\coloneqq(\lambda^{(t)},v^{(t)})
shows
Theorem

3.8
.
The key step of the proof is to establish the
smoothness
property for the KL divergence over the finite-dimensional space
\underline{\rm cone}(\mathcal{M};\bm{\alpha}\id)
, which is non-trivial given that the entropy functional is non-smooth over the full Wasserstein space
(see, e.g.,
30
, Section 6.2.2)
.

Computing the gradient.
To apply the optimization algorithms, we compute the gradient of
\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)
w.r.t.
(\lambda,v)
and the projection operator w.r.t.
\|\cdot\|_{Q}
. Under the change of variables formula,

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)=

\displaystyle-\int\log\det\Bigl({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}DT(x)\Bigr)\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\int V\Bigl({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})\,x+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}T(x)+v\Bigr)\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)+\textsc{const}\,.

Since
{\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}DT(x)
is always invertible, the partial gradients are computed as:

\displaystyle\nabla_{v}\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)=\int\nabla V\Bigl({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})\,x+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}T(x)+v\Bigr)\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)\,,

(92)

and

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda_{T}}\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)=

\displaystyle-\int\biggl[\tr\Bigl(\Bigl({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}DT(x)\Bigr)^{-1}\,DT(x)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\Bigl\langle T(x),\,\nabla V\Bigl({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})\,x+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}T(x)+v\Bigr)\Bigr\rangle\biggr]\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)\,.

(93)

To compute the first term in
\nabla_{\lambda_{T}}\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)
, note that
DT
is the following lower triangular matrix with non-negative diagonals:

[DT(x)]_{ij}=\begin{cases}\partial_{x_{i}}T_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})\,,&\text{if }j=i\,,\\
\partial_{x_{1}}T_{i}(x_{i};x_{1})\,,&\text{if }j=1\,,\\
0\,,&\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

The inverse of
{\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}DT(x)
can be efficiently calculated using forward substitution with
d
linear equations, each with at most two non-zero terms per row. This gives a total computational cost of
O(d\,|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|)
to compute all
DT(x)
terms and
O(d)
to invert it.

The terms involving
\nabla V
can be approximated by taking the averages of
\nabla V
evaluated at the samples drawn from the Gaussian distribution
\rho
.

Working toward proving
Theorem

C.18
, we collect some key facts about the matrix
\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}DT(x)
, including a bound on its spectral norm.

Lemma C.19
.

Fix
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Define
M(\lambda)\coloneqq\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}\,DT(x)
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\cup_{i=0}^{4}\mathcal{M}_{i}|}\times\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\prime}|}
. Then,
M(\lambda)
is lower triangular with non-negative diagonal entries and if
\|\lambda\|=1
, then
\|M(\lambda)\|_{2}^{2}\;\leq\;9\,\delta^{-2}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{j=0}^{4}\mathcal{M}_{j}\cup\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\prime}
. For each
T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, the Jacobian matrix
DT(x)
admits the following structure:

•

If
T\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
, the
(1,1)
entry of
DT(x)
is

\delta^{-1}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}\in[b,b+\delta)\}}
, and all the others are zero.

•

If
T\in\mathcal{M}_{1}
, the
(i,i)
entry of
DT(x)
is

\delta^{-1}\psi\bigl(\frac{x_{1}-b^{\prime}}{\delta}\bigr)\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{i}\in[b,b+\delta),\,x_{1}\in[b^{\prime},b^{\prime}+\delta)\}}
,
and the
(i,1)
entry is

\delta^{-1}\psi\bigl(\frac{x_{i}-b}{\delta}\bigr)\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{i}\in[b,b+\delta),\,x_{1}\in[b^{\prime},b^{\prime}+\delta)\}}
.
All other entries are zero.

•

If
T\in\mathcal{M}_{2}
, the
(i,i)
entry of
DT(x)
is

\delta^{-1}\psi\bigl(1-\frac{x_{1}-b^{\prime}}{\delta}\bigr)\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{i}\in[b,b+\delta),\,x_{1}\in[b^{\prime},b^{\prime}+\delta)\}}
,
and the
(i,1)
entry is

-\delta^{-1}\psi\bigl(\frac{x_{i}-b}{\delta}\bigr)\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{i}\in[b,b+\delta),\,x_{1}\in[b^{\prime},b^{\prime}+\delta)\}}
.
All other entries are zero.

•

If
T\in\mathcal{M}_{3}
, the
(i,i)
entry of
DT(x)
is

\delta^{-1}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{i}\in[b,b+\delta),\,x_{1}\geq R\}}
,
and all the others are zero.

•

If
T\in\mathcal{M}_{4}
, the
(i,i)
entry of
DT(x)
is

\delta^{-1}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{i}\in[b,b+\delta),\,x_{1}\leq-R\}}
,
and all the others are zero.

•

If
T\in\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\prime}
, the
(i,1)
entry of
DT(x)
is

\delta^{-1}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}\in[b,b+\delta)\}}
,
and all the others are zero.

By the structure of the maps in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, we conclude that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

[DT(x)]_{ii}\geq 0\,\,\text{for any }T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime},\quad\text{and }\quad\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}[DT(x)]_{ii}\leq\delta^{-1}\,.

(94)

For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\cup_{j=0}^{4}\mathcal{M}_{j}|}\times\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\prime}|}
, it follows that
M\coloneqq\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}DT(x)
is a lower triangular matrix with
M_{ii}\geq 0
.

To control
\|M\|_{2}
, we follow the argument as in
Lemma

C.17
to write
M=\mathbf{D}+ve_{1}^{\top}
where
\mathbf{D}
is a diagonal matrix and
v=(0,v_{2},\dotsc,v_{d})^{\top}
. Then,

\|M\|_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{D}\|_{2}+\|v\|\,.

(95)

By (
94
), we derive that for
\|\lambda\|=1
,

0\leq\mathbf{D}_{ii}\leq\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}[DT(x)]_{ii}\leq\delta^{-1}\,.

It remains to compute
\|v\|
. For each
i\geq 2
, if
x_{i}\in[-R,R)
and
x_{1}\in[-R,R)
, there exist unique
T_{1}^{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{1}
,
T_{2}^{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{2}
, and
T_{5}^{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\prime}
depending only on the sub-intervals of
(x_{1},x_{i})
such that

\max\bigl\{|[DT_{1}^{i}(x)]_{i1}|,\,|[DT_{2}^{i}(x)]_{i1}|,\,|[DT_{5}^{i}(x)]_{i1}|\bigr\}\leq\delta^{-1}\,,

and
[DT(x)]_{i1}=0
for any other
T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
. If
x_{i}\not\in[-R,R)
and
x_{1}\in[-R,R)
, there exists
T_{5}^{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\prime}
such that
|[DT_{5}^{i}(x)]_{i1}|\leq\delta^{-1}
and
[DT(x)]_{i1}=0
for any other
T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
. Otherwise, if
x_{1}\notin[-R,R)
, then
[DT(x)]_{i1}=0
for all
T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|v\|^{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{d}\Bigl(\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}\,[DT(x)]_{i1}\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{d}\bigl(\bigl[|\lambda_{T_{1}^{i}}DT_{1}^{i}(x)|+|\lambda_{T_{2}^{i}}DT_{2}^{i}(x)|+|\lambda_{T_{5}^{i}}DT_{5}^{i}(x)|\bigr]_{i1}\bigr)^{2}\leq 3\,\|\lambda\|^{2}\,\delta^{-2}=3\delta^{-2}\,.

Hence,
\|M\|_{2}\leq 3\delta^{-1}
.
∎

We now establish the geodesic smoothness result for the KL divergence over
\underline{\rm cone}(\mathcal{M};\bm{\alpha}\id)
. Recall that
\Upsilon\coloneqq\bigl(L_{V}^{\prime 1/2}+(d-1)\,L_{V}^{1/2}\bigr)^{2}\,\frac{9}{\delta^{2}}\,\|Q^{-1}\|_{2}
is defined in
Theorem

C.18
.

Lemma C.20
.

If
\pi
is
L
-log-smooth, the function
(\lambda,v)\mapsto\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)
is
(L+\Upsilon)
-smooth over the space
\left(\Theta,\|\cdot\|_{\Theta}\right)
.

Proof.

Denote by
M\coloneqq\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}DT(x)
. Recall the expressions (
92
) and (
93
) for the gradients of the KL divergence.

The second derivative of
\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)
w.r.t.
v
is

\nabla^{2}_{v}\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)=\int\nabla^{2}V\Bigl({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})\,x+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}T(x)+v\Bigr)\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)\,,

(96)

and the mixed partial derivatives w.r.t.
(\lambda,v)
are

\partial_{\lambda_{T}}\nabla_{v}\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)=\int\nabla^{2}V\Bigl({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})\,x+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}T(x)+v\Bigr)\,T(x)\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x).

(97)

The Hessian of
\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)
w.r.t.
\lambda
has entries:

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda_{T}}\partial_{\lambda_{T^{{}^{\prime}}}}\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)

\displaystyle=\int\biggl[\underbrace{\tr\Bigl(\bigl({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+M\bigr)^{-1}\,DT^{\prime}(x)\,\bigl({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+M\bigr)^{-1}\,DT(x)\Bigr)}_{\eqqcolon H_{T,T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\Bigl\langle T(x),\nabla^{2}V\Bigl({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})\,x+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}T(x)+v\Bigr)\,T^{\prime}(x)\Bigr\rangle\biggr]\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)\,.

To bound the operator norm of the matrix
H=(H_{T,T^{\prime}})_{T,T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}
, consider an arbitrary unit vector
u\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|}
with
\|u\|=1
. We compute

\displaystyle u^{\top}Hu

\displaystyle=\sum_{T,T{{}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}u_{T}\tr\bigl(({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+M)^{-1}\,DT^{{}^{\prime}}(x)\,({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+M)^{-1}\,DT(x)\bigr)u_{T^{{}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\tr\Bigl(({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+M)^{-1}\sum_{T^{{}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}u_{T^{{}^{\prime}}}DT^{{}^{\prime}}(x)\,({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+M)^{-1}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}u_{T}DT(x)\Bigr)\,.

For any lower triangular matrices
A,B\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that
B
has positive diagonal entries, the following hold
\tr(A^{2})\leq\tr(A)^{2}
and
|\tr(AB)|\leq\|A\|_{2}\tr(B)
. Together with
Lemma

C.19
, we derive that

\displaystyle u^{\top}Hu

\displaystyle\leq\tr\Bigl(({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+M)^{-1}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}u_{T}DT(x)\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tr\bigl(({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+M)^{-1}\bigr)^{2}\,\frac{9}{\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle=\tr\bigl(({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})+{\rm diag}(M))^{-1}\bigr)^{2}\,\frac{9}{\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\alpha_{j}^{-1}\Bigr)^{2}\,\frac{9}{\delta^{2}}=\bigl(L_{V}^{\prime 1/2}+(d-1)\,L_{V}^{1/2}\bigr)^{2}\,\frac{9}{\delta^{2}}\,,

where the last two lines follow by the property of lower triangular matrices and the non-negativity of the diagonal entries of
M
. Therefore, we have shown that

H\preceq L_{H}I_{|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|}\,,\qquad\text{where}\,\,L_{H}\coloneqq\bigl(L_{V}^{\prime 1/2}+(d-1)\,L_{V}^{1/2}\bigr)^{2}\,\frac{9}{\delta^{2}}\,.

(98)

Denote by
\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}=[T_{1},\ldots,T_{|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|}]:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|}
the matrix-valued function whose columns are transport maps in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.
By (
96
)–(
98
), we derive that

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\lambda,v}\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)

\displaystyle=\int\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}(x)\\
I\end{bmatrix}\nabla^{2}V\Bigl({\rm diag}(\bm{\alpha})\,x+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\lambda_{T}T(x)+v\Bigr)\left[\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}(x),I\right]\rho(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\qquad{}\phantom{=}+\begin{bmatrix}H&0_{|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|\times d}\\
0_{d\times|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|}&0_{d\times d}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\preceq L\int\left[\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}(x),I\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}(x),I\right]\rho(\mathrm{d}x)+L_{H}\begin{bmatrix}I_{|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|}&0_{|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|\times d}\\
0_{d\times|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|}&0_{d\times d}\end{bmatrix}\,.

Under the geometry
\left(\Theta,\|\cdot\|_{\Theta}\right)
, the partial Hessian
\nabla_{\lambda}^{2}\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)
needs to be rescaled by
Q^{-1/2}
. Concretely, for any linear operator
A:\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|+d}\to\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|+d}
, denote by
\|A\|_{\Theta}\coloneqq\sup\left\{\|A\theta\|_{\Theta}^{*}\,:\,\|\theta\|_{\Theta}=1\right\}
the operator norm of
A
where
\|\cdot\|_{\Theta}^{*}
is the dual norm of
\|\cdot\|_{\Theta}
, then
\|A\|_{\Theta}=\|G^{-1/2}AG^{-1/2}\|_{2}
with
G=\begin{bmatrix}Q&0_{|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|\times d}\\
0_{d\times|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|}&I\end{bmatrix}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\bigl\|\nabla^{2}_{\lambda,v}\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)\bigr\|_{\Theta}

\displaystyle\leq L\,\Bigl\|\int\left[\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}(x)\,Q^{-1/2},\,I\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}(x)\,Q^{-1/2},\,I\right]\rho(\mathrm{d}x)\Bigr\|_{2}+L_{H}\,\|Q^{-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=L\,\Bigl\|\begin{bmatrix}Q^{-1/2}\int\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}(x)^{\top}\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}(x)\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)\,Q^{-1/2}&0_{|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|\times d}\\[4.0pt]
0_{d\times|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|}&I\end{bmatrix}\Bigr\|_{2}+L_{H}\,\|Q^{-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq L+L_{H}\,\|Q^{-1}\|_{2}\,.

The second equality follows from the block structure of the matrix and the fact that

\int\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}(x)\,\rho(\mathrm{d}x)=0_{d\times|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|}
after re-centering.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

C.18
.

First, we apply
Lemma

2.6
: as
\pi
satisfies (
P1
) and (
RD

+

) implies (
RD
),
\pi
is
\ell_{V}^{\prime}\land\ell_{V}
-log-concave. Since (
R
) and (
P2
) are also satisfied,
\pi
is
L_{V}\lor(L_{V}^{\prime}/2)
-log-smooth.

By
Lemma

C.20
,
(\lambda,v)\mapsto\mathsf{KL}\left(\mu_{\lambda,v}\parallel\pi\right)
is
(\ell_{V}^{\prime}\wedge\ell_{V})
-strongly convex and
(L_{V}\vee(L_{V}^{\prime}/2)+\Upsilon)
-smooth over
(\Theta,\|\cdot\|_{\Theta})
.
The guarantee now follows from
(
11
, Theorem 10.29)
.
∎
